# 46 USB Type-C/USB Power Delivery interface (UCPD)

## 46.1 Introduction

The USB Type-C/USB Power Delivery interface complies with:

- Universal Serial Bus Type-C Cable and Connector Specification: release 2.4, Oct 2024
- Universal Serial Bus Power Delivery specifications:
<br>- revision 2.0, version 1.3, January 12, 2017
<br>- revision 3.2, version 1.1, October 2024

It integrates the physical layer of the Power Delivery (PD) specification, with CC signaling method (no VBUS), for operation with Type-C cables.

## 46.2 UCPD main features

- Compliance with USB Type-C specification release 2.4
- Compliance with USB Power Delivery specifications revision 2.0 and 3.2
<br>- Enabling advanced applications such as PPS (programmable power supply)
- Stop mode low-power operation support
- Built-in analog PHY
<br>- USB Type-C pull-up (Rp, all values) and pull-down (Rd) resistors
<br>- “Dead battery” Rd support
<br>- USB Power Delivery message transmission and reception
<br>-  FRS (fast role swap) Rx support
- Digital controller
<br>- BMC (bi-phase mark coding) encode and decode
<br>- 4b5b encode and decode
<br>- USB Type-C level detection with de-bounce, generating interrupts
<br>- FRS signaling
<br>- FRS detection, generating an interrupt
<br>- DMA-compatible byte-level interface for USB Power Delivery payload, generating interrupts
<br>- USB Power Delivery clock pre-scaler / dividers
<br>- CRC generation/checking
<br>- Support of ordered sets, with a programmable ordered set mask at receive
<br>- Clock recovery from incoming Rx stream

## 46.3 UCPD implementation

The devices have one UCPD controller to support one USB Type-C port.

In the following table, “X” denotes supported, and “-” not supported.

## 46.4 UCPD functional description

The UCPD peripheral provides hardware support for the USB Power Delivery control interface specification, using I/Os specifically designed for that purpose.

The built-in PHY directly detects Type-C voltage levels, supports Power Delivery BIST carrier mode 2 (Tx only), BIST test data (Tx and Rx), and Power Delivery Rx FRS signaling.


<div align="left" data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="527.78px"/><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="Body">For Power Delivery FRS Tx signaling, the device can be configured to control, through UCPD_FRSTX pin (alternate function), external NMOS transistors that ensure low-resistance pull-down on CC lines.</p></td></tr></table></div>


The UCPD transmitter BMC (bi-phase mark) encodes and transmits data: preamble, SOP, payload data from protocol layer (after 4b5b-encoding), CRC, and EOP on the Type-C connector CC lines. It automatically inserts inter-frame gap and executes “Hard Reset”.

The UCPD receiver detects SOP, BMC-decodes the incoming stream, recovers the preamble, 4b5b-decodes payload data, detects EOP, and checks CRC. It automatically detects five K-code SOP and two Reset ordered sets, plus two software-defined patterns (allows for only three out of four K-codes being correctly received, as defined by the standard).

In Stop mode, the peripheral maintains the ability to detect incoming USB Power Delivery messages and FRS signaling, which allows low-power operation.

### 46.4.2 UCPD reset and clocks

The peripheral has a single reset signal (APB bus reset).

The register section is clocked with the APB clock (ucpd\_pclk).

The main functional part of the transmitter is clocked with ucpd\_clk clock, pre-scaled from the ucpd\_ker\_ck clock according to the PSC\_UCPDCLK[2:0] bitfield of the UCPD\_CFGR1 register. The main functional part of the receiver is clocked with the ucpd\_rx\_clk recovered from the incoming bitstream.

The receiver is designed to work in the clock frequency range from 6 to 18 MHz. However, the optimum performance is ensured in the range from 6 to 12 MHz.

The following diagram shows the clocking and timing elements of the UCPD peripheral.

#### Figure 676. ![](MSv45536V3.svg)Clock division and timing elements

Refer to the USB PD specification in order to set appropriate delays. For tTransitionWindow and especially for tInterFrameGap, the clock frequency uncertainty must be taken into account so as to respect specified timings in all cases.

### 46.4.3 Physical layer protocol

The physical layer covers the signaling underlying the USB Power Delivery specification.

On the transmitter side its main function is to form packets according to the defined packet format including generally:

- preamble
- start of packet (SOP, ordered set)
- payload header
- payload data
- cyclic redundancy check (CRC) information
- end of packet (EOP)

Before going on the CC line, the data stream is BMC-encoded, respecting specified timing restrictions.

On the receive side, the principle task is to:

- extract start of packet (SOP, ordered set) information
- extract payload header
- extract payload data
- receive and check CRC
- receive end of packet (EOP)

The receive is basically a reverse of the transmit process, thus starting with BMC data stream decoding.

class="TableBodyC">Do Not Use</p></td></tr></table></div>

#### Bit ordering at transmission

Allowed transmission data units / data sizes are in the following table.

The bit transmission order is shown in the following figure.

#### Figure 678. Transmit order for various sizes of data![](MSv45538V1.svg)

#### Packet format

Messages other than Hard Reset and Cable Reset

The packet format is shown in the following figure, with information on 4b5b encode and data source.

<a id="XREF_40629__Table_9__Packet_format31"></a>

#### Figure 679. Packet format![](MSv45539V2.svg)

Hard Reset

The physical layer handles the Hard Reset signaling differently than the other types of message as it has higher priority to be able to interrupt an ongoing transfer.

The physical layer specification implies the following sequence in the case of an ongoing Tx message:

1. Terminate the message by sending an EOP K-code and discard the rest of the message.
2. Wait for tInterFrameGap time.
3. If the CC line is not idle, wait until it goes idle.
4. Send the preamble followed by the four K-codes of Hard Reset signaling.
5. Disable the CC channel (stop sending and receiving), reset the physical layer and inform the protocol layer that the physical layer is reset.
6. Re-enable the channel when requested by the protocol layer.

#### Figure 680. Line format of Hard Reset![](MSv45540V2.svg)

Cable Reset

Cable Reset shown in the following figure is similar in format to Hard Reset, but unlike Hard Reset it does not require a specific high-priority treatment.

#### Figure 681. Line format of Cable Reset![MSv45541V2](MSv45541V2.svg)

#### Collision avoidance

The physical layer respects the tInterFrameGap delay between end of last-transmitted bit of a Tx message, and the first bit of a following message.

It also checks the idle state of the CC line before starting transmission. The CC line is considered idle if it shows less than three (nTransitionCount) transitions within tTransitionWindow (12 to 20 μs). The Power Delivery specification revision 3.1 also requires to manage the Rp value (source) and monitor Type-C voltage level for these Rp modifications (at the sink).

#### Physical layer signaling schemes

The bit are signaled with bi-phase mark coding (BMC).

#### BIST

Depending on the BIST action required by the protocol layer, either of the following can be run:

- a Tx BIST pattern test, achieved by writing TXMODE and TXSEND
- an Rx BIST pattern test, achieved by writing RXMODE to the correct value for RXBIST.

The two possible patterns supported in UCPD (corresponding to “BMC” mode) are:

- BIST Test Data (192 bit pattern), applies to Tx and Rx. In the case of Rx, the message is received (but discarded rather than passing to the protocol layer, which must nevertheless still generate a GoodCRC Tx message in acknowledgment).
- BIST Carrier Mode 2 (single pattern, infinite length message), applies to Tx only. As opposed to Tx, the receiver in this mode simply ignores the CC line during this state.

BIST test data pattern

The test data pattern is not viewed as a special case in UCPD.

The BIST test data packet frame format is shown in the following figure.

#### Figure 682. BIST test data frame![MSv45542V2](MSv45542V2.svg)

This is a fixed length test data pattern. In reality the only aspect that marks its difference from the general packet format already shown in [Figure 679: Packet format](#XREF_40629__Table_9__Packet_format31) is the contents of the Header. As UCPD receives the Tx Header contents via programming (it is simply viewed as part of the payload), it is only this programming (and not the block’s behavior) that differentiates the general packet from the BIST Test Data packet.

BIST Carrier Mode 2

When required, this BIST test mode sends an alternating pattern of 1010 that is continually repeated. As this mode is intended for signal analysis it is stable condition with (in V1.0 of the USB PD specification) no defined length. Starting from V1.1 of the USB PD specification, the protocol layer defines a counter that indicates when to exit this mode.

The way to quit the infinite 1010 sequence (according to requirements of the USB PD specification) is to disable the UCPD peripheral via the UCPDEN bit.

#### Figure 683. BIST Carrier Mode 2 frame![MSv45543V2](MSv45543V2.svg)

### 46.4.4 UCPD BMC transmitter

The BMC transmitter comprises 4b5b encoding, CRC generation, and BMC encode, as shown in the following figure. Its output goes to the analog PHY through a channel switch.

#### Figure 684. UCPD BMC transmitter architecture![MSv45544V2](MSv45544V2.svg)

BMC encoder

The bi-phase mark coding method is defined in the IEC 60958-1 Digital Audio Interface Part:1 General Edition 3.0 2008-09 www.iec.ch specification.

The half-bit clock hbit\_clk is derived from ucpd\_clk through a simple divider controlled by the HBITCLKDIV[5:0] bitfield of the UCPD\_CFGR1 register. This ensures the same duration of high and low half-bit periods (if neglecting a small difference due to different rising and falling edge duration and due to jitter), and the same bit duration (if neglecting jitter).

Transmitter timing and collision avoidance

Hardware support of collision avoidance is made as a function of the half bit time for the transmitter. Two counters are implemented:

<br>- tInterFrameGap: via IFRGAP (pre-defined value, can be altered)
<br>- tTransitionWindow: via TRANSWIN (pre-defined value, can be altered)

These two counters once set correctly generates the interframe gap.

Hard Reset in transmitter

In order to facilitate generation of a Hard Reset, a special code of TXMODE field is used. No other fields need to be written.

On writing the correct code, the hardware forces Hard Reset Tx under the correct (optimal) timings with respect to an ongoing Tx message, which (if still in progress) is cleanly terminated by truncating the current sequence and directly appending an EOP K-code sequence. No specific interrupt is generated relating to this truncation event.

Transmitter behavior in the case of errors

The under-run condition (TXUND interrupt) may happen by accident and in this case, the UCPD is starved of (the correct) Tx payload and is not able to complete the Tx message correctly. This is a serious error (for this to happen the software fails to respond in time). As a result the hardware ensures the CRC is incorrect at the end of the message, thus guaranteeing the message to be discarded at the receiver.

### 46.4.5 UCPD BMC receiver

The UCPD BMC receiver performs:

- Clock recovery
- Preamble detection / timing derivation
- BMC decoding
- 4b5b decoding
- K-code ordered set recognition
- CRC checking
- SOP detection
- EOP detection

The receiver is activated as soon as the UCPD peripheral is enabled (via UCPDEN), but it waits for an idle CC line state before attempting to receive a message.

The following figure shows the UCPD BMC receiver high-level architecture.

#### Figure 685. UCPD BMC receiver architecture![](MSv45545V2.svg)

CRC checker

The received bits are fed into a CRC checker which evolves a 32-bit state during the received the payload bitstream. At the end the 32 bits of the CRC also fed into the logic

The EOP detection (5 bits) halts the process and a check is performed for the fixed residual state which confirms correct reception of the payload (in fact the residual is 0xC704DD78).

At this point the UCPD raises interrupt RXMSGEND. If the CRC was not correct then RXERR is set true and the receive data must be discarded.

Under normal operation, this interrupt would previously have been acknowledged and thus cleared. If this is not the case, a different interrupt RXOVR is generated in place of RXMSGEND.

Ordered set detection

This function detects the different ordered sets each consisting of four 5-bit K-codes.

Once we are in the preamble we opens a sliding window detection of the ordered set (4 words of 5 bits).

The ordered sets detected include all SOP\* codes (SOP, SOP’, and SOP’’), but also Hard Reset, Cable Reset, SOP’\_Debug, SOP’’\_Debug, and two extensions defined by registers UCPD\_RX\_ORDEXT1 and UCPD\_RX\_ORDEXT2.

EOP detection and Hard Reset exception handling

EOP is a fixed 5-bit K-code marking the end of a message.

The way in which a transmitter is required to send a Hard Reset (if a previous message transmit is still in progress) is that this previous message is truncated early with an EOP.

If Hard Reset were ignored, then the EOP detection can be done only at the expected time. However, due to the Hard Reset issue, the EOP detector must be active while an Rx message is arriving. When an “early EOP” is detected, the truncated Rx message is immediately discarded.

Truncated or corrupted message exception

Once the ordered set has been detected, depending on the message, there may be data bytes to be received which is completed with a CRC and EOP. If at any point during these phases an error condition happens:

- the line becomes static for a time significantly longer than one “UI” period (the exact threshold for this condition is not critical but the exception must occur before three UIs), or
- the message goes to the end but it is not recognized (for example EOP is corrupted).

In both cases, the receiver quits the current message, raising RXMSGEND and RXERR flags.

Short preamble or incomplete ordered set exception

In the exceptional case of the receiver seeing less that half of the expected preamble, the frequency estimation allowing correct BMC-decode becomes impossible. Even if the full preamble is seen, allowing frequency estimation, but the ordered set is not fully received before the line becomes static, the receiver state machine does not start.

In both of these cases, the clock-recovery/BMC decoder re-starts, checking initially for an IDLE condition, followed by a preamble, and then estimating frequency.

### 46.4.6 UCPD Type-C pull-ups (Rp) and pull-downs (Rd)

UCPD offers simple control of these resistors via ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0]. In case only one of the CC lines is to be used, it is possible to optimize power consumption by disabling control on the other line, through the CCENABLE[1:0] bitfield.

 


<div align="left"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="535.17px"/><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_deadbatt" height="96.00px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_deadbatt"><p class="Body">When the MCU is unpowered, it still presents the “dead battery” Rd<span data-conditional="_usbpd1_dbpins,_usbpd1_deadbatt">, provided that UCPDx_DBCC1 and UCPDx_DBCC2 pins are each connected to UCPDx_CC1 and </span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_dbpins,_usbpd1_deadbatt">UCPDx_CC2 pins, respectively.</span></p><p class="Body"><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_dbpins,_usbpd1_deadbatt">If dead battery behavior is not required (for example for source only products), then UCPDx_DBCC1 and UCPDx_DBCC2 pins must both be tied to ground</span>.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_deadbatt" height="40.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_deadbatt" style="display: none"><p class="Body">When the MCU is unpowered, it does not signal "dead battery" (which must therefore be handled with external components).</p></td></tr></table></div>


After power arrives and the MCU boots, the desired behavior (for example source) must be programmed into ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0] before setting the UCPD1\_DBDIS bit of the PWR\_CR3 register to remove dead battery pull-down resistor and allow the values just programmed to take effect.

Use of Standby low-power mode is possible for sinks in the unattached state.

### 46.4.7 UCPD Type-C voltage monitoring and de-bouncing

For correct operation of the Type-C state machine and for detecting the cable orientation, the CC1/2 lines must be monitored for voltage level, while ignoring fast events such as peaks.

Thresholds between voltage levels on the CC1/2 lines are determined through PHY threshold detector settings.

The TYPEC\_VSTATE\_CC1/2[1:0] bitfields reflect the CC1/2 line levels processed with a hardware de-bouncing filter that suppresses high-speed line events such as peaks. The PHYCCSEL bit selects the line, CC1 or CC2, to be used for Power Delivery signaling.

For minimizing the power consumption, it is recommended to use the polling method, with the Type-C detectors only turned on for the instant of polling, rather than keeping the Type-C detectors permanently on and wake the device up from Stop mode upon CC1/2 line events.

### 46.4.8 UCPD fast role swap (FRS)

#### FRS signaling

The FRS condition (a pulse of a specific length), is generated upon setting the FRSTX bit.


<div align="left" data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="535.90px"/><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="Body">For the duration of FRS condition, the I/O configured as UCPD_FRSTX (alternate function) controls, with high level, the gate of an external NMOS transistor that pulls the active CC line down.</p></td></tr></table></div>


#### FRS detection

FRS monitoring is enabled by setting the bit FRSRXEN, after writing PHYCCSEL that selects the active CC line depending on the cable orientation detected.


<div align="left" data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport"><table class="_Invisible"><col width="535.90px"/><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy,_usbpd1_frsrxsupport" height="56.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy,_usbpd1_frsrxsupport" style="display: none"><p class="Body">FRS monitoring works with both internal and external PHYs (dependent on USEEXTPHY). In the case of an external PHY, there are two inputs to the UCPD coming from elsewhere in the device (typically comparators).</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.4.9 UCPD DMA Interface

DMA is implemented in the UCPD and when it is enabled the byte-level interrupts to handle UCPD1\_TXDR and UCPD1\_RXDR registers (Tx and Rx data register, each one byte) are no longer needed.

By enabling bits TXDMAEN and/or RXDMAEN, DMA can be activated independently for Tx and/or Rx functionality.

### 46.4.10 Wake-up from Stop mode

For power consumption optimization, it is useful to use Stop mode and wait for events on CC lines to wake the MCU up.

In order for this to work, it must be first enabled by writing a 1 to WUPEN.

The events causing the wake-up can be:

- Events on the BMC receiver (RXORDDET, RXHRSTDET), hardware enable PHYRXEN
- Event on the FRS detector (FRSEVT), hardware enable FRSRXEN
- Events on the Type-C detectors (TYPECEVT1, TYPECEVT2), hardware enables CC1TCDIS, CC2TCDIS

## 46.5 UCPD programming sequences

The normal sequence of use of the UCPD unit is:

1. Configure UCPD.
2. Enable UCPD.
3. Concurrently:
<br>- On demand from protocol layer, send Tx message
<br>- Intercept (poll or wait for interrupt) relevant Rx messages and recover detail to hand off to protocol layer

Repeat the last point infinitely.

### 46.5.1 Initialization phase

Use the following sequence for a clean startup:

1. Prepare all initial clock divider values, by writing the UCPD\_CFG register.
2. Enable the unit, by setting the UCPDEN bit.

### 46.5.2 Type-C state machine handling

For the general application cases of source, sink, or dual-role port (the last alternating the source and the sink), the software must implement a corresponding USB Type-C state machine. The basic coding is in the following table.

data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">-</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vRa[1.5]</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vRd[1.5]</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vOPEN[1.5]</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">11: 3.0A Rp</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">-</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vRa[3.0]</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vRd[3.0]</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport,_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyC">vOPEN[3.0]</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyC">0:</p></td><td colspan="6" data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyC">Reserved</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">1: Sink</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">xx</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">-</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">vRa</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">vRd-USB</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">vRd-1.5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">vRd-3.0</p></td></tr></table></div>


The CCENABLE[1:0] bitfield can disable pull-up/pull-downs on one of the CC lines.

**Note:**

The Type-C state machine depends not only on CC line levels, but also on VBUS presence detection (sink mode) and, when in source mode, determines VCONN generation and VBUS state (ON/OFF/+voltage level); discharge). UCPD does not directly control VBUS generation circuitry nor VCONN load switch (enabling VCONN supply generator to be connected to the CC line), but the application needs these inputs and controls, to function correctly.

General programming sequence (with UCPD configured then enabled)

1. Set ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0] based on the current position in USB Type-C state machine (and also the current advertisement in the case of a source). This turns on the appropriate pull-ups/pull-downs on the CC lines, and defines the voltage levels that the TYPEC\_VSTATE fields represent. Note that before programming, the PHY is effectively off.
2. Read TYPEC\_VSTATE\_CC1/2 to determine the initial Type-C state (for example whether the local source is connected to a remote sink).
3. In the case of no connection, wait for a connection event.
4. Assuming a connection is detected and assuming a local Power Delivery functionality is implemented, start sending/receiving Power Delivery messages.
5. When a new interrupt/event occurs on PHYEVT1/2 indicating a change in stable voltage, re-evaluate the implications and give this input to the Type-C state machine.

Case of a source that needs to change between one of the three possible Rp values (Default-USB / 1.5A / 3.0A) and the sink connected to it:

- [Source] Simply reprogram ANASUBMODE[1:0]
- [Sink behavior from that time] PHYEVT1/2 occurs and the TYPEC\_VSTATE1/2 changes accordingly

Programming for a dual-role port (DRP) toggling from source to sink:

- Simply re-program ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0] to start the new behavior

Detailed programming sequence (example):

### 46.5.3 USB PD transmit

On reception of a message from the protocol layer (that is, to be sent), prepare Tx message contents by writing the UCPD\_TX\_ORDSET and UCPD\_TX\_PAYSZ registers.

The message transmission is triggered by setting the TXSEND bit, with an appropriate value of the TXMODE bitfield.

When the data byte is transmitted, the TXIS flag is raised to request a new data write to the UCPD\_TXDR register.

This re-iterates until the entire payload of data is transmitted.

Upon sending the CRC packet, the TXMSGSENT flag is set to indicate the completion of the message transmission.

#### Hard Reset transmission

As soon as it is known that a Hard Reset needs to be transmitted, setting the TXHRST bit of the UCPD\_CR register forces the internal state machine to generate the correct sequence. The value of UCPD\_TX\_ORDSET does not require update in this precise case (the correct code for Hard Reset is sent by UCPD).

The USB Power Delivery specification requires that in the case of an ongoing message transmission, the Hard Reset takes precedence. In this case, for example, UCPD truncates the payload of the current message, appending EOP to the end. No notification is available via the registers (for example through the TXMSGSEND flag). This is justified by the fact that the Hard Reset takes precedence over any previous activity (for which it is therefore no longer important to know if it is completed).

#### Use of DMA for transmission

DMA (Direct Memory Access) can be enabled for transmission by setting the TXDMAEN bit in the UCPD\_CR register.

For each message:

- Prepare the whole message in memory (starting with two header bytes)
- Program the DMA operation with a length corresponding to the two header bytes plus a number of data bytes corresponding to the number of data words multiplied by four
- Write TXSEND to initiate the message transfer
- If TXMSGDISC then go back to previous line (TXSEND)
- Wait for DMA transfer complete interrupt (that is, when last Tx byte written to UCPD)
- Double-check subsequent TXMSGSENT interrupt appears

### 46.5.4 USB PD receive

Notification of start of the receive message sequence is triggered by an interrupt on UCPD\_SR (bit RXORDDET).

The information is recovered by reading:

- UCPD\_RX\_SOP (on interrupt RXORDDET)
- UCPD\_RXDR (on interrupt RXNE, repeats for each byte)
- UCPD\_RXPAYSZ (on interrupt RXMSGEND)

The data previously read from UCPD\_RXDR above must be discarded at this point if the RXERR flag is set.

If the CRC is valid, the received data is transferred to the protocol layer.

For debug purposes, it may be desirable to track statistics of the number of incorrect K-codes received (this is done only when 3/4 K-codes were valid as defined in the specification). This is facilitated through:

- RXSOP3OF4 bit indicating the presence of at least one invalid K-code
- RXSOPKINVALID bitfield identifying the order of invalid K-code in the ordered set

#### Use of DMA for reception

DMA (Direct Memory Access) can be enabled for reception by setting the RXDMAEN bit in the UCPD\_CR register.

Whenever a Rx message is expected:

- Program a DMA receive operation (and spare buffer) a little longer than the maximum possible message (length depends on extended message support).
- After receiving RXORDDET, DMA operation starts working in the background.
- On reception of RXMSGEND interrupt, read RXPAYSZ.
- Double-check RXPAYSZ vs. the number of DMA Rx bytes (must correspond but DMA read of RXDR is slightly after RXDR gets last byte).
- Process the DMA Rx buffer.
- Prepare next Rx DMA buffer as soon as possible in order to be ready.

## 46.6 UCPD low-power modes

A summary of low-power modes is shown below in [Table 442: Effect of low power modes on the UCPD](#XREF_60529__Table_12__Effect_of_lower_power_modes_on_the_UCPD31).

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="1028529" style="margin-left : 0.00pt;text-align:center;">Table 442. <a id="UCPD31" name="XREF_60529__Table_12__Effect_of_lower_power_modes_on_the_UCPD31">Effect of low power modes on the UCPD</a></h4></caption><col width="106.84px"/><col width="431.75px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;text-indent : 56.69pt;"><a id="1028533" name="290_TableHeadC_1028533"></a>Mode</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC" style="margin-top : 0.00pt;margin-bottom : 0.00pt;text-indent : 56.69pt;">Description</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">Sleep</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">No effect</p></td></tr><tr height="37.33px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">Stop</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">Detection of events (Type-C, BMC Rx<span data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport">, FRS detection</span>) remains operational and can wake up the MCU.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_standby" height="37.33px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_standby"><p class="TableBodyC">Standby</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_standby"><p class="TableBodyC">UCPD is not operating, and cannot wake up the MCU. Pull-downs remain active if configured.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">Unpowered</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC"><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_deadbatt">Dead battery pull-downs remain active.</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


The UCPD is able to wake up the MCU from Stop mode when it recognizes a relevant event, either:

- Type-C event relating to a change in the voltage range seen on either of the CC lines, visible in TYPEC\_VSTATE\_CCx
- Power delivery receive message with an ordered set matching those filtered according to RXORDSETEN[8:0], visible by reading RXORDSET

Wake-up from Stop mode is enabled by setting the WUPEN bit in the UCPD\_CFG2 register.

At UCPD level three types of event requiring kernel clock activity may occur during Stop mode:

- Activity on the analog PHY voltage threshold detectors which can later be confirmed to be a stable change between voltage ranges defined in the Type-C specification
- Activity on Power Delivery BMC receiver (coming from the selected CC line) which can potentially generate an Rx message event (that is, RXORDSET) later
- Activity on Power Delivery FRS detector which can potentially generate an FRS signaling detection event (that is, FRSEVT) later

It order to function correctly with the RCC, the clock request signal is activated (conditional on WUPEN) when there is asynchronous activity on:

- Type-C voltage threshold detectors (coming from either CC line)
- Power Delivery receiver signal (from the selected CC line)
- FRS detection signal (from the selected CC line)

When an interrupt from the UCPD is received, then the software has to check what is the source of the interrupt by reading the UCPD\_SR register.

Depending on which bit is at 1, the ISR must handle that condition and clear the bit by a write to the appropriate bit of the UCPD\_ICR register.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UCPDEN</span>: UCPD peripheral enable</p><p class="TableIndent">General enable of the UCPD peripheral.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Upon disabling, the peripheral instantly quits any ongoing activity and all control bits and bitfields default to their reset values. They must be set to their desired values each time the peripheral transits from disabled to enabled state.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXDMAEN</span>: Reception DMA mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When set, the bit enables DMA mode for reception.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 29</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXDMAEN</span>: Transmission DMA mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When set, the bit enables DMA mode for transmission.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr height="198.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 28:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXORDSETEN[8:0]</span>: Receiver ordered set enable</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines the types of ordered sets that the receiver must detect. When set/cleared, each bit enables/disables a specific function:</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXXXXXXX1: SOP detect enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXXXXXX1X: SOP' detect enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXXXXX1XX: SOP'' detect enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXXXX1XXX: Hard Reset detect enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXXX1XXXX: Cable Detect reset enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXXX1XXXXX: SOP'_Debug enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bXX1XXXXXX: SOP''_Debug enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0bX1XXXXXXX: SOP extension#1 enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">0b1XXXXXXXX: SOP extension#2 enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="160.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PSC_UCPDCLK[2:0]</span>: Pre-scaler division ratio for generating ucpd_clk</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines the division ratio of a kernel clock pre-scaler producing UCPD peripheral clock (ucpd_clk).</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: 1 (bypass)<br/>0x1: 2<br/>0x2: 4<br/>0x3: 8<br/>0x4: 16</p><p class="TableIndent">It is recommended to use the pre-scaler so as to set the ucpd_clk frequency in the range from 6 to 9 MHz.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="145.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRANSWIN[4:0]</span>: Transition window duration</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines the division ratio (the bitfield value minus one) of a hbit_clk divider producing <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">tTransitionWindow</span> interval.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x00: Not supported<br/>0x01: 2<br/>0x09: 10 (recommended)<br/>0x1F: 32   </p><p class="TableIndent">Set a value that produces an interval of 12 to 20 us, taking into account the ucpd_clk frequency and the HBITCLKDIV[5:0] bitfield setting.</p></td></tr><tr height="189.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 10:6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IFRGAP[4:0]</span>: Division ratio for producing inter-frame gap timer clock</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines the division ratio (the bitfield value minus one) of a ucpd_clk divider producing inter-frame gap timer clock (<span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">tInterFrameGap</span>).</p><p class="TableIndent">0x00: Not supported<br/>0x01: 2<br/>0x0D: 14 <br/>0x0E: 15 <br/>0x0F: 16 <br/>0x1F: 32</p><p class="TableIndent">The division ratio 15 is to apply for Tx clock at the USB PD 2.0 specification nominal value. The division ratios below 15 are to apply for Tx clock below nominal, and the division ratios above 15 for Tx clock above nominal.</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 5:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">HBITCLKDIV[5:0]</span>: Division ratio for producing half-bit clock</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines the division ratio (the bitfield value plus one) of a ucpd_clk divider producing half-bit clock (hbit_clk).</p><p class="TableIndent">0x00: 1 (bypass)<br/>0x1A: 27<br/>0x3F: 64</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.2 UCPD configuration register 2 (UCPD\_CFGR2)

Address offset: 0x004

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Configuration of the UCPD Rx signal filtering. Writing to this register is only effective when UCPD is disabled (UCPDEN = 0).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:<span data-conditional="_nousbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol">4</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" height="70.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXAFILTEN</span>: Rx analog filter enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Setting the bit enables the Rx analog filter required for optimum Power Delivery reception.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="nonuser" height="40.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="nonuser" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:5</p></td><td data-conditional="nonuser" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CORRMANADJ[2:0]</span>: Manually adjust threshold for comparisons</p><p class="TableIndent">That is, S1&gt;(max(S2,S3)+CORRMANADJ). Expert mode only.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="nonuser" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="nonuser" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td data-conditional="nonuser" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CORRMAN</span>: Use manual adjustment to correlator. Expert mode only.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:4</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_rxfilt_intcontrol" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUPEN</span>: Wake-up from Stop mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Setting the bit enables the UCPD_ASYNC_INT signal.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FORCECLK</span>: Force ClkReq clock request</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Do not force clock request<br/>1: Force clock request</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFILT2N3</span>: BMC decoder Rx pre-filter sampling method</p><p class="TableBodyL">Number of consistent consecutive samples before confirming a new value.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: 3 samples<br/>1: 2 samples</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFILTDIS</span>: BMC decoder Rx pre-filter enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Enable<br/>1: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">The sampling clock is that of the receiver (that is, after pre-scaler).</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.3 UCPD control register (UCPD\_CR)

Address offset: 0x00C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CC2TCDIS</span>: CC2 Type-C detector disable</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit disables the Type-C detector on the CC2 line.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Enable<br/>1: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">When enabled, the Type-C detector for CC2 is configured through ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0].</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CC1TCDIS</span>: CC1 Type-C detector disable</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit disables the Type-C detector on the CC1 line.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Enable<br/>1: Disable</p><p class="TableIndent">When enabled, the Type-C detector for CC1 is configured through ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0].</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" height="130.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RPUSBABSENT</span>: Common external PHY drive mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set (common drive mode enabled), a single RPU1A5 control signal is used to drive external resistor ladders of both CC1 and CC2 lines. This allows optimizing the PCB design when Rp-USB is absent in the application. When the bit is cleared (common drive mode disabled), a separate drive signal is used for either CC line external PHY.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit is only significant for the operation with external PHY.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_sourcesupport" height="114.67px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_sourcesupport"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RDCH</span>: Rdch condition drive</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit drives Rdch condition on the CC line selected through the PHYCCSEL bit (thus associated with VCONN), by remaining set during the source-only <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">UnattachedWait.SRC</span> state, to respect the Type-C state. Refer to <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">"USB Type-C ECN for Source VCONN Discharge"</span>. The CCENABLE[1:0] bitfield must be set accordingly, too.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect<br/>1: Rdch condition drive</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_sourcesupport" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport" height="101.33px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frstxsupport"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRSTX</span>: FRS Tx signaling enable.</p><p class="TableIndent">Setting the bit enables FRS Tx signaling.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect<br/>1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit is cleared by hardware after a delay respecting the USB Power Delivery specification Revision 3.1.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frstxsupport" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frstxsupport" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frstxsupport" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport" height="85.33px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRSRXEN</span>: FRS event detection enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Setting the bit enables FRS Rx event (FRSEVT) detection on the CC line selected through the PHYCCSEL bit. 0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Clear the bit when the device is attached to an FRS-incapable source/sink.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frsrxsupport" height="22.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frsrxsupport" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_frsrxsupport" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span data-conditional="_nousbpd1_deadbaten">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" height="54.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CC2VCONNEN</span>: VCONN switch enable for CC2</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" height="54.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_vconnen_used" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CC1VCONNEN</span>: VCONN switch enable for CC1</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_vconnen_used"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" height="116.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_discrete_phy" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">USEEXTPHY</span>: USB Power Delivery transmitter analog PHY mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit determines whether the device uses the internal analog PHY for USB Power Delivery transmission or whether in requires an external PHY. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Internal PHY<br/>1: External PHY</p><p class="TableIndent">When cleared (internal PHY), the bit only enables the internal analog PHY on the CC line selected with the PHYCCSEL bit.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy" height="22.67px"><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td data-conditional="_nousbpd1_discrete_phy"><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="145.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CCENABLE[1:0]</span>: CC line enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield enables CC1 and CC2 line analog PHYs (pull-ups and pull-downs) according to ANAMODE and ANASUBMODE[1:0] setting.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: Disable both PHYs <br/>0x1: Enable CC1 PHY<br/>0x2: Enable CC2 PHY<br/>0x3: Enable CC1 and CC2 PHY</p><p class="TableIndent">A single line PHY can be enabled when, for example, the other line is driven by VCONN via an external VCONN switch. Enabling both PHYs is the normal usage for sink/source.</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ANAMODE</span>: Analog PHY operating mode</p><p class="TableIndent">0: <span data-conditional="_usbpd1_sourcesupport">Source</span><br/>1: Sink</p><p class="TableIndent">The use of CC1 and CC2 depends on CCENABLE. Refer to <a href="#XREF_29801__Table_15__Coding_for_ANAMODE%2C_ANASUBMODE_and_link_with_TYPEC31" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Table 440: Coding for ANAMODE, ANASUBMODE and link with TYPEC_VSTATE_CCx</a> for the effect of this bitfield in conjunction with ANASUBMODE[1:0].</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 8:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ANASUBMODE[1:0]</span>: Analog PHY sub-mode</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_29801__Table_15__Coding_for_ANAMODE%2C_ANASUBMODE_and_link_with_TYPEC31" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Table 440: Coding for ANAMODE, ANASUBMODE and link with TYPEC_VSTATE_CCx</a> for the effect of this bitfield.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PHYCCSEL</span>: CC1/CC2 line selector for USB Power Delivery signaling</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Use CC1 IO for Power Delivery communication<br/>1: Use CC2 IO for Power Delivery communication</p><p class="TableIndent">The selection depends on the cable orientation as discovered at attach.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PHYRXEN</span>: USB Power Delivery receiver enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Disable<br/>1: Enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Both CC1 and CC2 receivers are disabled when the bit is cleared. Only the CC receiver selected via the PHYCCSEL bit is enabled when the bit is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="145.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXMODE</span>: Receiver mode</p><p class="TableIndent">Determines the mode of the receiver.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Normal receive mode<br/>1: BIST receive mode (BIST test data mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set, RXORDSET behaves normally, RXDR no longer receives bytes yet the CRC checking still proceeds as for a normal message. As this mode prevents reception of the header (containing MessageID), software has to auto-increment a received MessageID counter for inclusion in the GoodCRC acknowledge that must still be transmitted during this test.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXHRST</span>: Command to send a Tx Hard Reset</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect<br/>1: Start Tx Hard Reset message</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit is cleared by hardware as soon as the message transmission begins or is discarded.</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXSEND</span>: Command to send a Tx packet</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No effect<br/>1: Start Tx packet transmission</p><p class="TableIndent">The bit is cleared by hardware as soon as the packet transmission begins or is discarded.</p></td></tr><tr height="146.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 1:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXMODE[1:0]</span>: Type of Tx packet</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing the bitfield triggers the action as follows, depending on the value:</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: Transmission of Tx packet previously defined in other registers<br/>0x1: Cable Reset sequence<br/>0x2: BIST test sequence (BIST Carrier Mode 2)</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: invalid</p><p class="TableIndent">From V1.1 of the USB PD specification, there is a counter defined for the duration of the BIST Carrier Mode 2. To quit this mode correctly (after the "tBISTContMode" delay), disable the peripheral (UCPDEN = 0).   </p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.4 UCPD interrupt mask register (UCPD\_IMR)

Address offset: 0x010

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1).

### 46.8.5 UCPD status register (UCPD\_SR)

Address offset: 0x014

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

The flags (single-bit status bitfields) are associated with interrupt request. Interrupt is generated if enabled by the corresponding bit of the UCPD\_IMR register.

VCC2 &lt; ~2.60 V<br/>11111: ~2.60 V &lt; VCC2</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span class="Italic" data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport" style="font-family : Arial ; ~0.2 V<br/>00001: ~0.2 V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~0.4 V<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">00011: ~0.4 V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~0.8 V<br/>00111: ~0.8 V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~1.60 V<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">01111: ~1.60V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~2.60 V<br/>11111: ~2.60 V &lt; VCC2</span></p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span class="Italic" data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Mask bits “x” when interpreting voltages from the connected device, as follows:<br/>ANAMODE = 0, ANASUBMODE[1:0] = 01 (Def-USB, CC line level interpretation)<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">x0xx0: VCC2 = vRa<br/>x0xx1: VCC2 = vRd<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">x1xx1: VCC2 = vOPEN<br/></span><span class="Italic" data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">ANAMODE = 0, ANASUBMODE[1:0] = 10 (1.5 A, CC line level interpretation)<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">x0x0x: VCC2 = vRa<br/>x0x1x: VCC2 = vRd<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">x1x1x: VCC2 = vOPEN<br/></span><span class="Italic" data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">ANAMODE = 0, ANASUBMODE[1:0] = 11 (3.0 A, CC line level interpretation)<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">0x0xx: VCC2 = vRa<br/>0x1xx: VCC2 = vRd<br/></span><span data-conditional="_usbpd1_show_raw,_usbpd1_sourcesupport">1x1xx: VCC2 = vOPEN</span></p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Sink mode </span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">(ANAMODE = 1)</span><span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:<br/></span>00000: VCC2 &lt; ~0.2 V (vRa)<br/>00001: ~0.2 V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~0.66 V (vRd - USB)<br/>00011: ~0.66 V &lt; VCC2 &lt; ~1.23 V (vRd - 1.5)<br/>00111: ~1.23 V &lt; VCC2 (vRd - 3.0)</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport" height="70.67px"><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td data-conditional="_usbpd1_frsrxsupport"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRSEVT</span>: FRS detection event</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag is cleared by setting the FRSEVTCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No new event<br/>1: New FRS receive event occurred</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TYPEC_VSTATE_CC2[1:0]</span>: CC2 line voltage level</p><p class="TableIndent">The status bitfield indicates the voltage level on the CC2 line in its steady state.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: Lowest<br/>0x1: Low<br/>0x2: High<br/>0x3: Highest</p><p class="TableIndent">The voltage variation on the CC2 line during USB PD messages due to the BMC PHY modulation does not impact the bitfield value.</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 17:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TYPEC_VSTATE_CC1[1:0]</span>: </p><p class="TableIndent">The status bitfield indicates the voltage level on the CC1 line in its steady state.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: Lowest<br/>0x1: Low<br/>0x2: High<br/>0x3: Highest</p><p class="TableIndent">The voltage variation on the CC1 line during USB PD messages due to the BMC PHY modulation does not impact the bitfield value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TYPECEVT2</span>: Type-C voltage level event on CC2 line</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates a change of the TYPEC_VSTATE_CC2[1:0] bitfield value, which corresponds to a new Type-C event. It is cleared by setting the TYPECEVT2CF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No new event<br/>1: A new Type-C event</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TYPECEVT1</span>: Type-C voltage level event on CC1 line</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates a change of the TYPEC_VSTATE_CC1[1:0] bitfield value, which corresponds to a new Type-C event. It is cleared by setting the TYPECEVT2CF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No new event<br/>1: A new Type-C event</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXERR</span>: Receive message error</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates errors of the last Rx message declared (via RXMSGEND), such as incorrect CRC or truncated message (a line becoming static before EOP is met). It is asserted whenever the RXMSGEND flag is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No error detected<br/>1: Error(s) detected</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXMSGEND</span>: Rx message received</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates whether a message (except Hard Reset message) has been received, regardless the CRC value. The flag is cleared by setting the RXMSGENDCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No new Rx message received<br/>1: A new Rx message received</p><p class="TableIndent">The RXERR flag set when the RXMSGEND flag goes high indicates errors in the last-received message. </p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXOVR</span>: Rx data overflow detection</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates Rx data buffer overflow. It is cleared by setting the RXOVRCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No overflow<br/>1: Overflow</p><p class="TableIndent">The buffer overflow can occur if the received data are not read fast enough.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXHRSTDET</span>: Rx Hard Reset receipt detection</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates the receipt of valid Hard Reset message. It is cleared by setting the RXHRSTDETCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Hard Reset not received<br/>1: Hard Reset received</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXORDDET</span>: Rx ordered set (4 K-codes) detection</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates the detection of an ordered set. The relevant information is stored in the RXORDSET[2:0] bitfield of the UCPD_RX_ORDSET register. It is cleared by setting the RXORDDETCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No ordered set detected<br/>1: A new ordered set detected</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXNE</span>: Receive data register not empty detection</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that the UCPD_RXDR register is not empty. It is automatically cleared upon reading UCPD_RXDR.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Rx data register empty   <br/>1: Rx data register not empty</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXUND</span>: Tx data underrun detection</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that the Tx data register (UCPD_TXDR) was not written in time for a transmit message to execute normally. It is cleared by setting the TXUNDCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Tx data underrun detected<br/>1: Tx data underrun detected</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">HRSTSENT</span>: Hard Reset message sent</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that the Hard Reset message is sent. The flag is cleared by setting the HRSTSENTCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Hard Reset message sent<br/>1: Hard Reset message sent</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">HRSTDISC</span>: Hard Reset discarded</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that the Hard Reset message is discarded. The flag is cleared by setting the HRSTDISCCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Hard Reset discarded<br/>1: Hard Reset discarded</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXMSGABT</span>: Transmit message abort</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that a Tx message is aborted due to a subsequent Hard Reset message send request taking priority during transmit. It is cleared by setting the TXMSGABTCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No transmit message abort<br/>1: Transmit message abort</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXMSGSENT</span>: Message transmission completed</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates the completion of packet transmission. It is cleared by setting the TXMSGSENTCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Tx message completed<br/>1: Tx message completed</p><p class="TableIndent">In the event of a message transmission interrupted by a Hard Reset, the flag is not raised.</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXMSGDISC</span>: Message transmission discarded</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that a message transmission was dropped. The flag is cleared by setting the TXMSGDISCCF bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Tx message discarded<br/>1: Tx message discarded</p><p class="TableIndent">Transmission of a message can be dropped if there is a concurrent receive in progress or at excessive noise on the line. After a Tx message is discarded, the flag is only raised when the CC line becomes idle.</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXIS</span>: Transmit interrupt status</p><p class="TableIndent">The flag indicates that the UCPD_TXDR register is empty and new data write is required (as the amount of data sent has not reached the payload size defined in the TXPAYSZ bitfield). The flag is cleared with the data write into the UCPD_TXDR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: New Tx data write not required<br/>1: New Tx data write required</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.6 UCPD interrupt clear register (UCPD\_ICR)

Address offset: 0x018

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1).

### 46.8.7 UCPD Tx ordered set type register (UCPD\_TX\_ORDSETR)

Address offset: 0x01C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1) and no packet transmission is in progress (TXSEND and TXHRST bits are both low).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXORDSET[19:0]</span>: Ordered set to transmit</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield determines a full 20-bit sequence to transmit, consisting of four K-codes, each of five bits, defining the packet to transmit. The bit 0 (bit 0 of K-code1) is the first, the bit 19 (bit 4 of K-code4) the last.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.8 UCPD Tx payload size register (UCPD\_TX\_PAYSZR)

Address offset: 0x020

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="173.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 9:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXPAYSZ[9:0]</span>: Payload size yet to transmit</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield is modified by software and by hardware. It contains the number of bytes of a payload (including header but excluding CRC) yet to transmit: each time a data byte is written into the UCPD_TXDR register, the bitfield value decrements and the TXIS bit is set, except when the bitfield value reaches zero. The enumerated values are standard payload sizes before the start of transmission.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x2: 2 bytes - the size of Control message from the protocol layer<br/>0x6: 6 bytes - the shortest Data message allowed from the protocol layer)<br/>0x1E: 30 bytes - the longest non-extended Data message allowed from the protocol layer<br/>0x106: 262 bytes - the longest possible extended message<br/>0x3FF: 1024 bytes - the longest possible payload (for future expansion)</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.9 UCPD Tx data register (UCPD\_TXDR)

Address offset: 0x024

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is enabled (UCPDEN = 1).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="129.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 6:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXSOPKINVALID[2:0]</span>: </p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield is for debug purposes only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: No K-code corrupted<br/>0x1: First K-code corrupted<br/>0x2: Second K-code corrupted<br/>0x3: Third K-code corrupted<br/>0x4: Fourth K-code corrupted<br/>Others: Invalid</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXSOP3OF4</span>: </p><p class="TableIndent">The bit indicates the number of correct K-codes. For debug purposes only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: 4 correct K-codes out of 4<br/>1: 3 correct K-codes out of 4</p></td></tr><tr height="142.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXORDSET[2:0]</span>: Rx ordered set code detected</p><p class="TableIndent">0x0: SOP code detected in receiver<br/>0x1: SOP' code detected in receiver<br/>0x2: SOP'' code detected in receiver<br/>0x3: SOP'_Debug detected in receiver<br/>0x4: SOP''_Debug detected in receiver<br/>0x5: Cable Reset detected in receiver<br/>0x6: SOP extension#1 detected in receiver<br/>0x7: SOP extension#2 detected in receiver</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.11 UCPD Rx payload size register (UCPD\_RX\_PAYSZR)

Address offset: 0x02C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="174.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 9:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXPAYSZ[9:0]</span>: Rx payload size received</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield contains the number of bytes of a payload (including header but excluding CRC) received: each time a new data byte is received in the UCPD_RXDR register, the bitfield value increments and the RXMSGEND flag is set (and an interrupt generated if enabled).</p><p class="TableIndent">0x2: 2 bytes - the size of Control message from the protocol layer<br/>0x6: 6 bytes - the shortest Data message allowed from the protocol layer)<br/>0x1E: 30 bytes - the longest non-extended Data message allowed from the protocol layer<br/>0x106: 262 bytes - the longest possible extended message<br/>0x3FF: 1024 bytes - the longest possible payload (for future expansion)</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield may return a spurious value when a byte reception is ongoing (the RXMSGEND flag is low).</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.12 UCPD receive data register (UCPD\_RXDR)

Address offset: 0x030

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

### 46.8.13  UCPD Rx ordered set extension register 1   (UCPD\_RX\_ORDEXTR1)

Address offset: 0x034

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is disabled (UCPDEN = 0).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXSOPX1[19:0]</span>: Ordered set 1 received</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield contains a full 20-bit sequence received, consisting of four K-codes, each of five bits. The bit 0 (bit 0 of K-code1) is receive first, the bit 19 (bit 4 of K-code4) last.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 46.8.14  UCPD Rx ordered set extension register 2   (UCPD\_RX\_ORDEXTR2)

Address offset: 0x038

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Writing to this register is only effective when the peripheral is disabled (UCPDEN = 0).

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="112.59px"/><col width="501.58px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXSOPX2[19:0]</span>: Ordered set 2 received</p><p class="TableIndent">The bitfield contains a full 20-bit sequence received, consisting of four K-codes, each of five bits. The bit 0 (bit 0 of K-code1) is receive first, the bit 19 (bit 4 of K-code4) last.</p></td></tr></table></div>
